Agenda item — Proposal for a rebalancing of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia for the year 2015. The proposal for a budget rebalancing was submitted by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to present the proposal for a rebalancing of the budget for 2015, I give the floor first to the President of the Government, Dr. Mir Cerar.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u2	Honourable President of the National Assembly, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! In a relatively short period of time, the government has also prepared the National Assembly for consideration and has now proposed a second proposal for a budget rebalancing. In November, the National Assembly considered and adopted the 2014 budget balance and, at the end of January 2015, the Government submitted to it a proposal for a budget rebalancing for 2015. All this intensity in the preparation of the budgets' rebalancing reflects the serious intention of the Government and the National Assembly to take care of sustainable public finances now and in the future, thus fulfilling one of the important assumptions for the successful financial, economic and, ultimately, social development of society. Let me recall, however, that from September to today, in addition to the measures for fiscal consolidation, we have already prepared several amendments to the laws, have actively taken some remedial measures regarding last year's floods, have successfully negotiated and negotiated with representatives of various social spheres and groups, and have finally concluded a social agreement with the social partners. Of course, all of this is just the beginning, for we have many challenges and challenges ahead. We have recently witnessed the recovery of the Slovenian economy. After the recession, we expect an estimate that growth for 2014 has risen sharply above 2% of GDP. Unemployment, which was 9.6% in November 2014, is declining. The downward trend is also expected in the future. The export share, which is the main factor in economic growth, remains high. Despite current encouraging forecasts of economic growth and better labour market indicators, the economic and social situation in the country remains extremely demanding. While an optimistic forecast of economic growth does not yet mean that we have long-term and economic growth in Slovenia, further action is needed in various areas. In particular, appropriate measures in the area of public finances are needed. With the continued, excessive deficit of long-term and stable economic growth, which can provide us with a quality level of social state, we will not achieve it. Public finances are thus now at the heart of our attention and are forcing us to take decisive action. Only long-term sustainable public finances can provide us with a solid basis for stable economic growth, with further political stability, effective rule of law and, finally, a stable and adequately active banking sector. Slovenia, as a member of the EU and in particular as a member of the European Monetary Union, is committed to respect common rules and restrictions. The basic orientations, together with the objectives, already give us a common strategy for growth and jobs called the Europe 2020 Strategy. This framework also includes the process of the European Semester of Economic Governance, which has been exposed to the cycle of economic and fiscal policy coordination in the European Union. On the basis of this cycle, Member States must align their budgetary and economic policies with the objectives and rules agreed at European Union level. As part of the European Semester, Slovenia must also take into account the Council's recommendations on measures and reforms in the field of public finances, banking, the labour market, business governance, the business environment and the broader approach to preventing macroeconomic imbalances. In the area of corporate governance, the debt and restructuring process, they run parallel, with the process of improving the management of the country's capital investments and increasing their efficiency, including deliberate privatisation. At the same time, the government is planning further measures to increase competitiveness, reduce administrative barriers and improve the efficiency of the public sector. Slovenia has been in the excessive deficit process since November 2009, which means it must lead fiscal policy by ensuring a sustained and sustainable reduction of the country's deficit below 3% of GDP in 2015. For long-term development-oriented public finance policy is essential, clearly limiting public financial expenditure. Planning and setting targets is important. We must be aware that we must not spend more than we create in the medium and long term. The government and the National Assembly have already taken the first step in the right direction by adopting and implementing the 2014 state budget rebalancing, as we have achieved the country's projected deficit of 3.4% of GDP, following a preliminary assessment, without taking into account one-off measures. With the 2015 proposal for a state budget rebalancing, we want to continue with responsibility and determination in this direction and to achieve the objective to which we committed ourselves in the 2014 Stability Programme and, as we say, to reduce the country's deficit below 3% of GDP. With the revised structure of the proposed budget rebalancing, the government follows two key objectives: firstly, promoting economic growth, and secondly, increasing employment. The objectives are also pursued by all other systemic solutions and measures, thus creating the conditions for a certain coordination of pensions and social transfers for the next year after a long period of time. We have also committed ourselves to this by the coalition parties in our agreement. The changed circumstances both in the macroeconomic environment and due to legislative changes have contributed to the necessity of preparing a proposal for a rebalancing. We have taken this as a challenge and, as an important opportunity, as well as as a state budget, we have focused heavily on investment, which will contribute to further growth. The remaining European funds available to Slovenia from the past financial perspective will also contribute greatly to this. As far as possible, we have avoided radical interventions in individual parts of the system, as we do not want to cause less demand or consumption through fiscal policy restrictiveity. We have sought and adopted various sectoral and horizontal measures, both on the income side and on the expenditure side, which are jointly sustainable for all sides. This has also worked through long-standing and for all sides arduous negotiations, which have led to important compromises and many good solutions through constructive and open dialogue and joint efforts. We all had to accept compromises. In order to reach an agreement on the final proposal for the budget rebalancing, all ministers or ministries also contributed a significant share of the reduction in their respective areas. Nevertheless, we will be implementing many investment projects this year. Among other things, we will invest in the completion of emergency centres across Slovenia, invest in the construction of certain sections of the railway line, improve the water supply system in some parts of Slovenia, we will spend some €80 million for flood safety, and, among other things, we will create a state computer cloud, which we want to save on long-term resources for the state information system, if I only mention some things. Dear Members and Members! If we want to get out of the crisis in the long term, we still need to respond to the unstable situation as uniform, as connected and economically strong as possible. I believe that we are on the right track and that the proposed budget rebalancing for 2015 is an important part of this path. Let me reiterate that this balance is necessary, with the aim of achieving the commitments made while at the same time stimulating the economy and employment. Let us not forget, however, that it is not only our own commitments to the European, that is, our legal order, because the consolidation of public finances and the cessation of further indebtedness are essential, primarily for the normal and effective management of their own country. This is our key commitment to citizens and citizens and future generations. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u3	In order to present the proposal for the 2015 budget rebalancing, I also give the floor to Dr Dusan Maramor, Minister of Finance.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u4	Dear Members and Members! You have before you the proposal for a budget rebalancing for 2015. It is the seventh year after the start of the crisis, and I must say, after all the rebalances and budgets that I have accepted or struggled with in my life, it has been far more difficult. As soon as the government began its mandate, it is said that in September last year, we started preparing for this rebalancing, so it took almost five months. There were very hard, difficult negotiations in between. And we need to understand that this is probably one of the most difficult rebalancings that we will accept, because we expect that we are at the bottom and that from here on out we will have economic growth and that there will be a crisis behind us. This budget also follows this objective. With this proposal, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia continues the policy of balancing public finances, as already stressed in the Prime Minister's speech. The Government also wants this document to continue the path to stable and sustainable economic growth. In particular, I would like to stress that without economic growth it is not possible to achieve a balance of public finances, at least not sustainable. The three main objectives are set out in this document. The first objective is to draw 100% of European funding for the old financial perspective 2007-2013. The second objective is to reduce the country's deficit below 3% of GDP according to ESA standards in 2015, by which the government committed itself to the Stability Programme 2014 and the maximum public financial support for gross domestic product growth, i.e., maximum focus on investment and material costs. The 2015 budget rebalancing was urgently needed, primarily because the revenue projections in the adopted budget were based on the macroeconomic baselines of the 2013 Umarje autumn forecast. As you know, in 2013 a significant difference was predicted, such as today's situation, not only in the projections of Umarje, but also in all international institutions. These starting points, of course, are now substantially changed. In forecasting revenue in the preparation of the 2015 budget rebalancing proposal, we have taken into account the preliminary implementation for 2014 and this new macroeconomic forecast from Umara's winter forecast of economic trends, which predicts 2% of economic growth for 2015, but at the same time lowers the forecast of price growth. I would like to stress this in particular because price increases will be extremely low, practical growth is only real growth, but of course the budget is made on a nominal basis. In addition, changes in circumstances and factors of some major groups of revenue and expenditure also affected the trends of public financial objectives. The main derogations stem from the loss of property tax revenues, the unplanned debt interest expenditure effected after the 2015 budget, the under-planned ongoing transfer to the ZPIZ due to the adoption of the new Slovenian State Holding Act in spring 2014, which introduced changes in the financing of the regular expenditure of the Kad pension fund and the changes in the situation with regard to EU funds. The planned budget deficit for 2015 in the adopted budget was 2.4% of gross domestic product, but these changes, which I mentioned and some others, because we did not accept structural changes, but we only accepted temporary budget changes, would lead to a 5% deficit if we had a so-called spontaneous scenario. It was therefore necessary to introduce measures that brought this 5% deficit below 3% of gross domestic product. If we look at it this way, let's say just a drop in property taxes and an increase in interest, they contribute to about 1 percent of the additional deficit. Therefore, all these measures, already mentioned by the Prime Minister, were necessary for the period up to 31. 12. The adoption of laws, the adoption of various amendments to other regulations in order to comply with these rules of the Stability and Growth Pact in 2015, and for the first time since the start of the crisis have a deficit of less than 3 % of gross domestic product. Allow me to briefly present to you the specific estimates of revenue and expenditure. The 2015 draft budget rebalancing totals €8 billion 562 million in revenue, which is less than €64 million in the 2015 budget adopted. The maximum drawdown is estimated on the tax revenue side, which is, of course, due to the failure to enforce the real estate tax law, but the Government has taken several horizontal and sectoral measures that will ease this drop: first maintaining the fourth income class, then raising the rate of the financial service tax, increasing the rate of the insurance tax, increasing environmental taxes due to CO2 emissions, increasing the efficiency of the collection of public taxes, changes in the student labour system and other measures. Some of these measures are only extended from the previous period, some of them are replaced by some that are no longer in force. We are therefore planning EUR 6 billion 870 million of tax revenue in the draft budget rebalancing, which is still about 300 million more than such revenues were realised in 2014, which is, according to 2014, EUR 578 million of non-tax revenue and EUR 40.7 million of capital revenue, donations and transfer revenue. We are also planning a billion €72 million of the EU budget received. The proposal for a rebalancing provides for expenditure, the right side of expenditure, totalling EUR 9 billion 947 million, which is more than 461 million in the approved budget and 292 million more than in 2014. The proposal for a rebalancing is on the expenditure side, as already mentioned, aimed at providing for legal obligations and 100 % of EU funding, and therefore the maximum increase in expenditure is intended for the use of EU funds and associated Slovenian participation. With regard to integrated expenditure, it should be stressed that these are falling by EUR 50 million in relation to the implementation of 2014, which is justified by the measures already approved by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia at the meeting on 22 October in order to ensure a total reduction in expenditure in other areas. Some of these measures include: centralisation of public procurement, centralisation of state information technology, measures to reduce labour costs, measures in the area of co-financing of municipalities, rationalisation of economic public services, replacement of certain non-refundable subsidies in return and other sectoral measures by the ministries. The expenditure cut shows that of the total of €9.9 billion of expenditure, EU spending is €1 billion 308 million, with Slovenia participating €229 million, i.e. 15.5% of the expenditure side is linked to EU spending, since 2015 is the last year when we can still draw on the previous financial perspective 2007-2013. The programme classification, which is important, shows that compared to the past year's consumption, according to 2014, significantly more resources are allocated to the rebalancing of programmes and commitments, i.e. 134 % more, environmental protection and environmental infrastructure by 67 % more, and health care by 23 %. In this context, I would like to point out that the 2015 rebalancing target is markedly investment, as I have already mentioned, as compared to 2014 spending, the purchase and construction of core funds is increasing by 41% and investment transfers by 30%, i.e. investment expenditure totalling around 30%. Let me list some major investment projects that have not yet been mentioned by the Prime Minister. From the rail transport area the reconstruction of the railway line Divača-Koper, reconstruction of the railway Prague-Hodoš. This is a relatively high expenditure of 100 million 57 million. Upgrading of the railway section of Slovenska Bistrica–Pragersko and so on. From the area of roads of Škofja Loka, the national programme of motorways. In the areas of water supply and wastewater treatment, projects are planned on the Soča river, Upper Sava, Mure, Ljubljana and so on. In the field of information society, larger projects: the establishment of a state computer cloud, the construction of central information infrastructure, the establishment of optical connectivity for educational and research institutions and, for example, some other major projects that we know: Planica Ski Centre, Gymnastics Center Ljubljana. This proves that the 2015 budget rebalancing proposal actually follows the objective of increasing investment and, as we know, increasing investment works multiplely on GDP growth and therefore such a restructuring of the budget. Among the other expenditure, I should specifically point out the interest that, despite the nominally higher debt situation than in the previous year, due to the environment of low interest rates in 2015 and, of course, due to the decrease in Slovenia's relative interest rates vis-à-vis other countries, due to the return on good access to financial markets, these interest rates will be lower by 35 million than in 2014. The nominally lower labour costs will also be in line with an agreement with the unions, the subsidies will be lower due to the diversion to reimbursement. There is a slight decrease in the volume of funds allocated to transfers to individuals and households, but the reason for the reduction is not in the reduction of social transfers rights, but in the context of the reduction in the number of beneficiaries of parental benefits and the financial compensation for unemployment, due to economic growth and better employment. If I move on to the deficit area. The proposal for a rebalancing 2015 foresees a deficit in the state budget after the cash flow. After the cash flow, we are also working on a budget of €385 million in Slovenia, which amounts to 3.62% of GDP. After a correction to the accounting flow, in particular part of the EU's funds retained by the European Commission until the final report on the effectiveness of EU funds drawback for the period 2007-2013, and taking into account the expected balances of other units of the country's sector, we expect the country's deficit under the ESA methodology in 2015 to be below 3 % of GDP. In our own words, we will invest in getting them back later from the European Commission. This assumption of these funds increases the deficit, the cash flow deficit compared to the deficit in the accounting methodology used by Europe to measure, or we comply with the rules of the Stability and Growth Pact and the fiscal pact. Your Honor, allow me to re-emphasize. While the proposal for a rebalancing increases expenditure on the adopted budget for 2015, it does reduce resources in a wide range of areas. In doing so, we cannot all be satisfied, as each of us has contributed to the rationalisation of our area, but the adoption of the 2015 budget balance represents a further step towards the goal of balancing public finances. The rebalancing is also a strong investment focus in 2015, which will contribute to the promotion of economic growth and to sustainable public finances over the long term. Slovenia has already implemented certain measures on the path of consolidation of public finances in the past, but additional measures and consistent implementation of those measures are needed to ensure long-term and sustainable growth, and I would like to stress here the mantra which is highlighted in the European Union, and this is structural reforms. It takes into account all of the above, I propose to the National Assembly that the proposal for a rebalancing be adopted. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u5	The draft budget balance was considered by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy as the parent working body. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to the President of Mrs Urska Ban.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u6	Thank you for your word. Hello, everyone! The Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy is at its 19th emergency session. 2. 2015 as a parent working body considered the draft rebalancing of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia for 2015 submitted to the National Assembly for consideration by the Government. The Committee was provided with opinions from interested labour bodies, which took note of the proposal for a rebalancing and did not adopt their amendments and the opinion of the Commission of the State Council for the Economy, Crafts, Tourism and Finance, which supports the proposal for a rebalancing. During the course of business, 41 amendments were tabled by qualified applicants. Representatives and representatives of the Ministry of Finance, the Government, representatives of Umar and the Syndicate of Education, Education, Science and Culture and the Police Union attended the meetings of the Committee. The initial explanation of the balance presented was provided by the State Secretary of the Ministry of Finance, who gave an accurate account of the reasons for the submission of the balance for 2015. In doing so, she presented estimates of revenue and expenditure in the 2015 budget rebalancing proposal, where the government is planning €8 billion 562 million in revenue. The expenditure of the state budget provides for expenditure totalling EUR 9 billion 947 million, which means more than €461.8 million than in the budget adopted and 292 million more than in 2014. In the proposal for a budget rebalancing compared to last year's spending, a substantial number of funds are allocated to intervention programmes and commitments for environmental protection and infrastructure, local self-government and health care. The proposal for a rebalancing is investment-oriented, as compared to 2014 consumption, the purchase and construction of basic funds is increasing by 41% and investment transfers by 20%. The bulk of this increase is at the expense of investments linked to European funds. This structure of government budget revenue and expenditure means that the proposal for a rebalancing foresees a deficit in the state budget following a cash flow of €1 billion 385 million, which amounts to 3.62% of GDP. The government thus estimates that the total deficit of the state sector according to the ESA methodology in 2015 will be by 3% of GDP or 2,89% more precisely. The Government’s assessment is that, given the circumstances, the proposed balance is appropriate and constitutes a good basis for the implementation of the budget or for public funding in 2015. The Director of the Office for Macroeconomic Analysis and Development briefly presented the general economic picture for 2014 and said that the proposal for a rebalancing was based on the expected economic developments that were drawn up in Umar last December. In the debate on the tabled rebalancing, some Committee members stressed that the proposal for a 2015 budget rebalancing was unrealistic, that it was not in line with the fiscal rule enshrined in the Constitution and that it was not development-oriented. Not enough efforts have been made on the revenue side. It is also supposed to be designed on the wrong priorities and is therefore not socially oriented. Some considered that it was only the end of the development cycle of the previous government, and highlighted as problematic, in particular, the under-budgeted resources for the Ministry of the Interior and the Ministry of Health. In particular, they highlighted the issue of 11 unfinished cohesion projects in connection with municipalities which have difficulties due to uncertainty about the decisions to be taken. The issue of financing learning assistance to children with special needs and insufficient resources for police operations, while ensuring human security and property, was also highlighted. On the other hand, some members felt that the budget rebalancing was following the public financial objectives of correcting the surplus deficit in a sustainable manner, gradually reducing the structural deficit of the state sector and thus allowing for long-term sustainability of public finances. They therefore consider it appropriate to accept it as proposed. The Committee also identified itself to the amendments tabled and supported the amendment of the coalition's parliamentary groups. Following the discussion, the Committee agreed that the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy supported the proposal for a rebalancing of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia for 2015. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u9	The text is given by the Group of Social Democrats, Mr Matjaž Han.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u10	Thank you very much, Mr. President. The President of the Government, the Council of Ministers, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! In the beginning, a nice greeting and let me say that the Social Democrats Group will accept the document, which is the most important, which is the budget balance. Under the circumstances of the consideration of the 2015 budget rebalancing proposal, we should, respectably, translate into some moderate optimism. For the first time since the start of the public financial crisis, we have the tangible hope of ending the period of a savings stamp imposed by political elites apparently squeezed into so-called financial markets. After years of falling economic activity, the situation has improved for the second year in a row and the indicators show that Slovenia is on average being strengthened by the Member States of the European Union. If our 2015 prospects were still insufficient and strong below the euro area average, we are now exceeding the average estimated by the European Commission. Gross social product is expected to increase by 1.8% and, according to the Office for Macroeconomic Analysis and Development, by even 2%, while other euro area countries are expected to be experiencing less growth, i.e. the 1.3% circus. Social Democrats are particularly encouraged by the forecast that the growth of gross social product will also be followed by a further decline in the unemployment of persons. The growth of social production will continue to be based on exports in the future, on household consumption, which increased for the first time since 2010, and, of course, on private sector investments, while underlining that the contribution of public investment due to the expiry of the financial perspective of the European Union will be somewhat modest. For Slovenia, an export-oriented country, it is important to assess the European Commission that European integration has not been in such good shape since 2007. At the same time, we look forward to improving the growth forecast in Germany as our most important non-trade partners. Modesty growth is expected to trigger quantitative release – you know, this is the Draghi system – and the investment plan and the fall in oil prices. It seems that the European Union is unfortunately somewhat late, but for the first time, a vision of economic and social recovery based on tangible assumptions. In the context of these baselines, the Government has drawn up a proposal for a 2015 budget rebalancing which adapts the amount of items from the end of 2013 to a real improvement. Nevertheless, in the circumstances imposed in particular by the repayment of public debt and commitments to Brussels, the Government insists on public financial sustainability, which is consequently reflected in the further limitation of spending in certain areas. The Group of Social Democrats welcomes the part of the proposal for a rebalancing which preserves the social standard achieved and expresses its concern for the socially weakest part of society. Among the encouraging messages of the proposal for a rebalancing are in particular the reduction in the number of beneficiaries of social transfers as a result of the obvious improvement in the economic environment. We are also satisfied with the increase in investments of 41%, which means that we can finally expect the decomposition of the hat in which the investment in Fat Rtič has become entangled. After 13 years of efforts, the construction of a new promotion, training, work and protection centre will begin there in 2015. Regardless of the fate of some of the amendments tabled to the proposal for a rebalancing, we expect the Government with internal transfers to provide the resources necessary to carry out the legislative tasks or to prevent difficult remedial consequences. In particular, juggling, respected, between the ministries regarding the provision of funds for the payment of teaching aid to the Social Democrats, is considered to be an unfair or irresponsible conduct which the Government is obliged to go beyond. ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u12	Mr President, thank you for the floor. Dear Prime Minister, distinguished ministerial team, respectable colleagues and colleagues! The budget and the balance ahead are based on the belt tightening policy. In a policy which, after three years, also becomes clear to the European Commission that this policy is wrong, it has recently announced the flexible application of the Maastricht rights and has therefore not acted against France, which has made it clear that it will not reduce the deficit below 3% by 2017 at least. At that time, the European Central Bank has finally announced the purchase of state bonds, which it should have done in 2010, as did the US and Japanese central banks. In these times, the Slovenian government does not have the courage to set itself 3% for the budgetary objective, namely the Maastricht criteria. We are again more papal than the pope, and the Slovenian government wants a deficit of 2.89%. This gave up 36 million euros that could be used for development and social affairs, but the government will rather see the rate of poverty and material deprivation increase as it stands up for its citizens in Brussels. Slovenia can now be more indebted than Italy or Spain; interest rates are at historically record low rates. At the same time, the European Commission has announced that it will look at countries through its fingers at public expenditure on investment. The courageous government should therefore, in agreement with the European Commission, propose a development and social budget. There could also be a deficit of 3% if expenditure were incurred for the right purposes, i.e. for investment and development. In these times, the Government of Mira Cerar offers us a recycling of the savings budget of the former government, which was created in significantly different times. This budget was prepared at a time when Slovenia was the victim of a speculative attack in the financial markets, at a time when speculation was everywhere about who would be the next victim of the troika. Today, however, circumstances are completely different. If GDP fell by 2.8% in 2012 and by an additional percentage in 2013 and reached 7% of the bonds in those years, today our people and the economy have achieved 2.5% economic growth, one of the highest rates in the euro area. So our economy, our people and our country together create more than they consume. We have already had a budget surplus since 2009. In 2013, our surplus in foreign exchange was EUR 2 billion and EUR 100 million. In 2014, when the final calculations arrive, this number will be exceeded. So we create more than we consume. The main question that the government should ask itself is how this product is distributed among the population. Respected, macroeconomic conditions in Slovenia and the political situation in Europe have changed radically. And even more could it have been if Minister Marble had recently supported these efforts in the negotiations of the Eurogroup with Greece, which is working towards the end of the savings and the new investment cycle in Europe, rather than beating them short-sightedly. However, there is no more excuse to insist on the savings budget. The citizens of this country deserve and need such a budget. How about him? The social and development budget first requires a fair burden distribution on the revenue side. The budget and the balance before us are again based on unfair tax policy. The government reduces the deficit only by cutting spending and rights, but nothing has been done on the revenue side. On the revenue side, we have reserves that governments do not want to take advantage of, as this would extend to the privileges of capital and the most powerful. This is also reflected in Eurostat's annual production and comparison of European countries' tax policies. This analysis shows where the reserves are. Firstly, we often hear that we have the highest taxes in Slovenia. That's not true. Slovenia is on average under the European Union's tax revenues, with 35.7% of taxes collected in the EU, an average of 39.4% of taxes. Not to mention the more developed Member States in the European Union. The conclusion is that our governments simply do not collect enough taxes and contributions for the normal functioning of a modern country. Secondly, we often hear that we have some sort of balance sheet in Slovenia. That's not true either. From 2006 onwards, the tax rates for the assets are decreasing, the exemption is only the introduction of 50% of the income class, and from the same year, 2006 onwards, on the other hand, the VAT rate has doubled. After spending, we are at the European summit and therefore have an over-average share of indirect taxes, with almost 39% of us, 34.5 in the European Union, i.e. taxes that are socially least fair, while at the same time the richest capital is under-averagely taxed. Thirdly, Slovenia has the minimum tax base for capital income taxes among all members of the European Union. Slovenia has a share of capital tax and capital income in all tax revenues in the last place in the European Union. The average member collects 21% of tax revenues from this title of capital taxes; in Slovenia only 10. Last year, the government, I quote a draft rebalancing, say and write, collected 1.9 million taxes on assets. So there is a lot of salt in Barbara Hren's recent article in Dnevnik, which raised so much dust because he put the thesis that Slovenia is becoming a tax haven for wealth. So what should we do? In Slovenia, there are only two ways to reach the development and social budget – with additional debt or with a fair tax reform. In the United Left, of course, we claim that the right path to the social and development budget is a fair tax reform that will contain the following solutions. First, higher taxes on capital. As I said, in Slovenia, capital taxes are among the lowest in Europe. Secondly, the overall progressive taxation of all natural persons' incomes, not only labour income, but also capital and property income, must also be included in the progressive scale, and the 2006 Bajuk reform, which has separated and introduced the cedual taxation of dividends and other incomes, must be abolished. Thirdly, a social tax on real estate based on the actual value of real estate and which will additionally tax the high-end and luxury property, including real estate and luxury properties of religious communities. Fourthly, a special tax should be imposed on the property of a great deal of knowledge. Fifthly, it is necessary to prevent money from leaking into tax havens. If this is not possible, at least the statutory tax evasion of financial transactions in these countries is legal, we know which they are. Only with a fair increase in the tax will we be able to reach a development, investment and social budget that will not be based on a permanent debt. There's no other way. Slovenia needs a budget in which people and development will be priorities. However, the draft budget we have before us is moving in frameworks that are wrong. It is made with an artificial limit of 3%, based on a tax policy that does not want to collect taxes from capital owners and the richest, but also within these wrong frameworks the government has not set a good priority in terms of needs. The priorities are set here by inertia or depending on how much power each coalition partner has. The priorities of the budget and the rebalancing should, in the view of the United Left, be three. First, health, secondly, decent life, and thirdly, the development of each individual as well as the development of society and economy. If I stop by everyone's house for a moment. First time. Hello. The proportion of funds allocated to health care in Slovenia is low and falling. In 2013, it fell to 8.8% of GDP. The additional problem is that we have a very low share of public expenditure on health. According to Eurostat, public health expenditure in Slovenia is 7% of GDP and only 6.5% of Slovenia’s Health Insurance Institute is estimated. If we want our people to have such health care that is comparable to the developed world, it is necessary to increase budgetary expenditure on health and finally abolish supplementary health insurance. In Austria, public health funds account for 2% more than we do, i.e. 8.5% of GDP, with Austria twice as much GDP as Slovenia. Such policies should also be followed by our country. Another time. Social security. According to the United Left, social protection should be one of the priorities. We should ensure a decent life for everyone. After the share of social expenditure, we are again below average. For us, these expenditure amounts to 18.9%, 20% in the European Union as a whole and 21.4% in Austria. This budget consists of two thirds of the cost of living calculated six years ago. So not only does this budget not allow people on the social side of a decent life, but the question is whether people who depend on social assistance are allowed physical survival at all. Third time. Development. What kind of development can we even talk about when €10 million is allocated to research and development in the economic sector. Expenditures for republics and Zois scholarships are reduced by 30 million with some unconvincing promises that the government will get another 10 million. This year's scholarships will go down by a third. The funds for research projects are significantly too low, and investment is chronically lacking. The government obviously forgets about development. So where are the reserves? As I have already mentioned, the largest reserves would be in fair taxation and the reserves are also in the redistribution of expenditure according to social needs. I'm only going to point out one case because I don't have much time to defend myself. We must ask ourselves whether we can afford a defence system that is no longer suitable for the defence of the country, but only for military operations abroad. This is not only an assessment of the United Left, but it is also confirmed by the doctrine of the military strategy – the 2012 military strategic reserve of the Republic of Slovenia; I quote: "The Slovenian armies of its peaceful capabilities allow for successful participation in international operations and missions of NATO, the EU and the UN, but to an existing extent only partially appropriate and insufficient to ensure the full defence of national territory." Since when is it normal for a country that is not under military threat to have seven soldiers on one reserve? I'll give you an answer right away. This is normal since our political class joined NATO. Many times it is said that we have very low spending on defence, but look, in 2012, we had 1.1% of GDP to defend, Austria is spending 0.7%. Why? Because it's a neutral state with an army based on a reserve structure, because they don't allow themselves to buy weapons as badly as, say, modernise the T-55S tanks, which we've been costing a lot of modernisation, that they're now being preserved in some warehouse. How, then, can national defence be provided rationally? It has to be based on the conclusion that, for the first time, Slovenia is not militarily threatened, and for the second time, that we have written in the constitution to advocate a policy of peace and non-violence. So there is no reason for Slovenia to remain a member of NATO. For Slovenia, a non-blocking position is appropriate, but neutrality can also follow the example of Austria or Finland. Therefore, it is now necessary to turn the expensive army of professionals into an army based on reserves. This can be done organically, without any forgiveness, simply by not replacing members who leave the permanent structure. According to MORS, the Slovenian Army has left 3 000 400 in the last 10 years, 998 have retired, and the remaining 2 000 400 have left because of the contractual termination of the employment relationship and contracts for a certain period of time. So about as much as the permanent composition of the Slovenian Army counts, 7 000 200 soldiers have left it in the last 10 years. This means that in 10 years, Slovenia can reduce its permanent composition and replace it with a spare composition that will be 3- to 4-fold cheaper. This, on the one hand, will provide significant savings and, on the other hand, we will disperse the obligation among the population and ensure a more effective defence of the national territory if ever necessary. Additional savings arise if we adapt the "family composition and equipment of the army to the self-defence capabilities of the Slovenian territory" by quoting the doctrine of strategic military reserve instead of constantly adapting it to the needs of NATO and to the ambitions of the officers. As it is said, with such measures, we would spend three to four times as much money in defense as we do now. Let me slowly move on to the conclusion. Slovenia remains a prisoner of two development paradigms that lead to a dead end. The right alternative is neoliberalism. It is intended to reduce taxes and state expenditure, introduce a majority electoral system and another republic, thus strengthening the political power to resist the reduction of social and labour rights that have driven out the last governments. The alternative offered by the so-called middle and left, the last three governments, is neoliberalism with the human face. This in the area of public finances means a lighter fiscal rule, slightly fewer cuts in wages and slightly fewer cuts in social rights. But when we look around in four years' time, we will see that Mr Cerar and his team slowly brought us to the same other republic, under the well-known principle that if you throw the frog into hot water, jump out of the pot, if you slowly heat the water, it will not be noticed and cooked. The real alternative advocated by us, the left, is a fair tax reform, a social and development-oriented budget. But this is not in this budget, so unfortunately we cannot support it. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u19	The word is given by the SMC Group, for which Dr. Simona Kustec Lipicer.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u20	Dear President! Dear Council of Ministers! Dear colleagues and colleagues! The State budget represents the financial backbone of each country and is a fundamental instrument for the implementation of its macroeconomic policy. A healthy budget is balanced between the expenditure and income sides and is primarily in line with trends in socio-economic and not calendar cycles. The macroeconomic stability and a balanced budget with stable public finances are reflected through reduced interest rates, increased savings and investments and the creation of trade surpluses. On such grounds, it is possible to further promote both fair and balanced social and economic development for the diverse social groups. All these views in the event of an understanding of the state budget in the Republic of Slovenia already have several cycles, either calendar or political, do not hold water. In other words, they do not meet the needs and expectations of sound and stable state financial support for the public interests of diverse social and economic groups in the country. Even today's 2015 budget rebalancing does not have the power of a miracle stick that could instantly remove our country's current budgetary image and condition. The balance sheet under consideration is not the primary result of the current government's public financial vision, as it is prepared on the established foundations of the past government. However, in the SMC Group, we agree with the government that in the given situation this balance is the result of the best possible compromise. We give our support for the rebalancing for two large sets of reasons where one relates to the role and responsibility of democratically elected authorities to the people and the other to the substantive programme vision of this central financial and political document of the country. In the SMC Group, we believe that, with an intervention in the form of a rebalancing already in November 2013, the 2015 budget has been adopted with a responsible, fully binding and, above all, a timely response from the State to the changing domestic and international macroeconomic situation, we are re-examined in a professional and political manner the balance and stabilisation of the State's public finances, and consequently the development opportunities and an enabling socio-economic environment. Furthermore, in the SMC Group, we support the adoption of the budget rebalancing because the result is both within the government and the result of a broad social dialogue signed by the Government with key stakeholders. In this context, it is appropriate to re-emphasize successful agreements with the social partners and municipalities at home and the positive and supportive responses of the competent institutions of the European Union, whose full-fledged Member with all its rights and obligations is our country. The preparation and adoption of the budget balance is not least reflected in the signs of stabilisation and normalisation of the situation in our country. In the last turbulent years, the events that we have had an impact on ourselves in the dominant work, and in part the wider global financial crisis, the circumstances and conditions for the smooth realization of our country's social and economic objectives have changed significantly, to worse. Today is neither the time nor the space nor, finally, the need to debate the political, legal, social and values-moral brakes of our country's uninterrupted and in the future development of a focused operation, but rather the financial and programme content and direction of the document itself. Although this does not in any way exclude the heavy burden of responsibility that the first-mentioned reasons have for the public financial situation. In terms of content, the proposed 2015 budget rebalancing follows the principles of a sustainable and supportive social state, economic growth and gives particular weight to the investment potential. An accurate view of changes in budgetary resources between the initially adopted budget and the rebalancing of the basic pillars of the system shows unchanged or even partially increased financial values. For example, this shows data on pension protection, education and sport, transport and transport infrastructure, environmental and environmental infrastructure protection, public debt servicing and taking into account the lower unemployment rate, and thus the need for state transfers to individuals and households also on social security items. The programme classification of the budget also shows that, compared to the expenditure of the previous year, a substantial number of resources are allocated to health care, intervention programmes and environmental and infrastructure protection obligations, as well as to the field of local self-government. Furthermore, a detailed expenditure cut shows that the Government, through measures to reduce spending on the integral part of the budget, has been able to make progress by designing a series of measures that operate horizontally, i.e. on all budgetary users. In particular, the centralisation of public procurement and state information technology and the creation of an IT cloud, the centralisation of public property management, measures to reduce public sector labour costs, measures in the areas of co-financing of municipalities, changes in the student labour system, rationalisation of economic public services and the replacement of certain grants for reimbursement. Others are also important, however, measures that operate sectorally, i.e. through individual ministries, where ministries themselves have the opportunity to take certain measures on the basis of the financial framework provided, either internally redistributing or ideally reducing the costs of their operations. In particular, the investment expenditure category of the State budget should also be highlighted at this point. On the one hand, the rebalancing also had to be made due to the initial unforeseeable losses of property tax revenue, the unplanned interest on debt, the under-planned current transfer to the ZIPZ due to the adoption of the novel SDH Law, which was adopted in the spring of 2014, by way of a tactic by the same government, which also adopted the original budget for 2015. On the other hand, when we talk about this work, this change in the balance has also been linked to the previously mentioned changed circumstances of EU funds drawback. Why is this so important? Because it is precisely these that will provide a significantly higher opportunity for public investment. These increase by 30% or almost €357 million after the 2015 budget rebalancing compared to 2014. This represents 5.5% of gross social product in 2015, the highest share in recent years and is about 3% above the average of public investment in the euro area and the European Union area. This is particularly true of the logically related expectations that will result in positive effects of investment on economic growth, employment and social security, particularly on the overall future development orientation of the country in which the highest possible level of public responsibility will also have to be played by processes related to the strategic management of state assets and the well-thought and cost-effective sale of non-strategic investments of the country. At this point and in the light of the exposure, it is also worth mentioning the latest forecasts of economic trends for Slovenia, which foresee that real economic growth at 25 gross social product levels will continue to decrease by 4.3% at the end of January 2015 to 9.5% less than a year ago, which is also noticeably lower than the average of 11.2% of the unemployment rate in the euro area countries. And finally. The proposal for a rebalancing foresees that the country's general sector deficit under the ESA methodology will still amount to an acceptable and publicly sustainable 2.89% of the gross social product. In the SMC Group, we estimate that the objectives of the public financial policy resulting from the proposed balance sheet follow both the links to our citizens and citizens as well as the demands and recommendations of the European Council and the commitments of the 2014 stability programme as well as the draft 2015 budget plan. The rebalancing was prepared at a time and situation where the country still faces a major deficit and the consequences of the global economic crisis, which were so extensive that they were in real danger of the final destruction of the Slovenian social and economic model, the destruction of social cohesion and also the complete mistrust of people that the political institutions and processes of the Slovenian State were even able to continue the way of the independent state. In the SMC Group, we believe that, under the circumstances presented, the rebalance meets the expectations and needs of protecting the social and economic interests of people who, at these difficult times, especially need support and confidence in the country and its services. The same is true of the country's attitude towards businesses or the promotion of healthy public and no longer of partial and perverse entrepreneurial interests and practices. The country is rightly expected to offer support for the transition from the crisis and the conditions of healthy competitiveness, which will ultimately benefit people under equal conditions and incentives once again, which will further reflect the predictability of their employment opportunities and social security. In the SMC Group, we reiterate that we understand the 2015 budget rebalancing as an urgent and certainly unpopular act that we must, like the key social and local stakeholders of this country, have already done in dialogue with the government, responsibly confirm if we are to remedy the consequences of a series of unimaginable and unsuccessful past practices in the functioning of the country and in the management of public finances. Only in this way will we be able to provide the reformed framework of the rehabilitated system, on the basis of which it will be possible to build a healthy, fresh and, above all, for the benefit of future social and economic development, a sound foundation for a stable and stimulating public financial system and in this context a comprehensive development model of the country that its people will be able to restore to them at this moment a well-deserved and justifiable trust in its mission and existence. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u42	Thank you so much for saying that. Allow me to respond to a few discussions in the first round of the general debate on the budget balance. This budget balance, like any budget balance or baseline budget, is prepared on the basis of macroeconomic assessments prepared by the competent official institutions of the country. According to the law, it is always necessary to take into account the latest public forecast of macroeconomic indicators, and according to this starting point this budget balance is also based on the Office for Macroeconomic Analysis and Development winter forecast – Umar, which predicts 2% economic growth for this year. True, it is an assessment, but the assessments of other institutions are quite similar. For example, in its winter forecast of Slovenia, the European Commission is forecasting 1.8% of economic growth this year. There's something else. All of these estimates have proved to be those where the gap between the actual realisation we are looking at in relation to the official data provided by the Statistical Office and the smallest estimate. For this reason, we estimate that the starting points on which the proposal for a budget rebalancing is based are realistic and actually feasible this year, as regards economic growth and the reduction of the number of unemployed or the growth of employment in the country. It is also very important for the preparation of the rebalancing, as the Minister has already mentioned in the introduction, for the assessment of Death as regards inflation this year. A key stimulus to economic growth must be a stable fiscal framework and such a fiscal consolidation that will not prevent growth in consumption; this is also the focus of the budget rebalancing. If I go a little bit further on specific questions. First on the revenue side. Estimates of income growth in this year are the result of actual income estimates in 2014. The basis is an assessment of employment growth and a forecast of wage developments in the private and public sectors. In the field of excise duties, it is also crucial to take into account that the trend of decreasing sales of excise duties in the second half of last year has calmed down and can be relied on for stable consumption or even for a certain increase in consumption in terms of projected economic growth, particularly in the area of energy consumption. In the area of dividends, we expect, in this year's year's income to be approximately the same as that achieved last year, and we estimate that this estimate is realistic. On the expenditure side, there are also some highlights. First of all – what about the savers of the Ljubljana Bank in Bosnia and Croatia? This year, we are obliged, in accordance with the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights, to prepare the legal basis for the payment of these damages. Preparation of legal bases is under way. Framework assessments have also been made on how many such payments can be expected, but these payments depend on the course of concrete procedures to be determined by law. As a result, large payments calculated on the basis of the cash flow are not expected this year and for these payments are provided under the Succession Fund. More attention will have to be paid in the coming years. As regards the assessment of the expenditure referred to in this Title under the accounting flow, however, expenditure was recorded in 2014 during so-called one-off events or one-off financial effects. As a result, we do not expect any negative adjustments to this year's cash-flow deficit accounted for by the accounting principle. Expenses for municipalities. The fact is that we have agreed in negotiations with the municipalities to reduce the average, which provides municipalities with the means to fulfil their legal obligations. The fact is that we have agreed to reduce investment transfer under Article 21 of the Law on the Financing of Municipalities from 22 million to 11 million. At the same time, however, we have agreed to implement measures that will reduce the costs for the municipalities; and the first set of measures is already on the table today or will be dealt with tomorrow. This is a set of measures included in the intervention law. For other measures that we have agreed with the congregations that we need a little more time, it is agreed to put them into effect by 30. 6. However, from the perspective of the budget rebalancing and from the point of view of the investment cycle warnings in municipalities, it should be noted that this rebalancing of the budget for municipalities provides 487 million investment transfers; not only 11 million under Article 21 of the Law on Community Financing, but 487 million. Last year's realisation was 410 million. Most of these investment funds are at the expense of completing the old financial perspective, where municipalities bear a great deal of weight in cohesion projects; but nevertheless, these are the funds provided to municipalities for their investment activities. The question was raised regarding eleven cohesion policy projects that have failed to run for the use of funds from old cohesion, which are agreed in cooperation with the municipalities, in order to give priority to the implementation of the new financial perspective. Seven of these projects are subject to formal conditions in order to be able to implement them or to be implemented already; they will be provided with funding as soon as the formalities for drawing on the European funds of the new financial perspective are completed. For four projects, the opinion of the European institutions must also be obtained because of their environmental impact. The resources necessary to enable this year to carry out what is necessary in relation to these projects are provided by the European Cohesion Policy Office, the Water Management sub-programme and the Climate Change Management sub-programme; a total of €24 million. This is indeed less than the value of these eleven projects, but these are the resources needed to start projects this year, as all eleven projects are for such activities that cannot be carried out in one calendar year. The amount of interest that we have to pay this year is not a reflection of the current borrowing procedures, but a reflection of all the debt we have so far. These interest rates amount to around 40 million less than the required interest payments in last year. This reduction is based on the fact that several debts with higher interest rates were repaid in last year or this year; and refinancing can be provided at lower interest rates. With regard to social transfers, I should point out that the scope of social transfers is comparable to that of 2014. It is true that the estimated consumption in these areas in the budget balance is significantly lower than in the budget adopted, but we must not forget that the changed economic situation has a strong impact on the expenditure under this heading. As has been pointed out in the introduction by several discussers, this reduction is not at the expense of reducing or any additional restriction on the rights to social transfers; it is due to the changed situation, and therefore also to the reallocation between different types of transfers. With regard to the grant funds. Unfortunately, Mr. Mesco isn't with us, but I believe he's listening. During the discussion, he assessed spending exclusively on the use of integral resources, where a good 50 million grant funds were foreseen. However, he did not take into account that there are still €37.4 million in the grant budget fund. In the area of integral resources, less resources are foreseen for this year with a rebalancing than in the adopted budget; however, we must take into account at the same time the realisation of these expenditure in 2014, which amounted to 45,5 million on the part of the integral resources. However, higher expenditure from the budget fund was foreseen than in this year. As a result, we are adding more resources from integrated funds this year, in view of the realisation in 2014. With regard to warnings concerning the fiscal rule or the compatibility of this budget balance with the Constitution. The government's assessment is that this proposal for a rebalancing is in line with the constitutional fiscal rule, and we are also taking very seriously the commitment to confirm the fiscal rule in the first reading at the January meeting of the National Assembly. As a result, we are directing our own attention or great efforts to prepare the amendments in time for the Fiscal Rule Act to be discussed at the March Assembly. Other information about investments. It was mentioned that investment is indeed increasing, but that the base is small. Once again, in 2014, investment and investment transfers were earmarked for a billion EUR 100 million of budget resources of all kinds, earmarked, integrated and European, representing 11.4% of total budget expenditure. This year we are earmarking €550 million for investments, representing 15.6% of the total state budget. With regard to the expenditure for young people mentioned, I should mention that, as a number of other areas mentioned in the debate, additional resources could be added to each area if public funding were allowed. Thus, a balance must be found between what is desirable and what is possible. Let me just point out that the Youth Office had a million 809 million expenditure in last summer. This year, we are increasing their expenditure to a million 826 million. At the same time, of course, it must be borne in mind that these are not the only expenditure earmarked for this area. For example, only under the same programme is the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport allocated €31 million. So much in this circle. Thank you very much.

We certainly gain a large share by raising funds. At the Ministry of Defence, we have decided to rationalise our business by disinvestment in the field of arms, real estate and a large proportion of these assets through disinvestment and rationalisation. The second source of funds that contributed to the increase in the budget is already mentioned in today's debates - the European Union Solidarity Fund, to which we are entitled, due to the damage caused to us by last year's sting. This is just a transfer and it goes to citizens or to local communities. And the third source, which contributed to an increase in the nominal amount, is the European crisis management project, where we applied and received this project. When we talk about the increase, we are talking about the means of use and not about the integral means. Of course, the movement of defence expenditure should also be taken into account. We are an integral part of the NATO alliance and there is also an important amount of defence spending that we are earmarking for defence. I must say, and you all know this, that the defence expenditure from 2010 to today fell very quickly and in large quantities. I must say that defence expenditure was between 2010 and 1.6% of gross domestic product. They then decreased by 1.05 % in 2013. Last year we have barely reached a percentage, and in 2015, as defined by this balance, we will fall below a percentage, to 0.97% of the defence expenditure in gross domestic product. This is also our problem when we communicate with our allies. You know very well that the Alliance has a commitment to allocate about 2% of the gross domestic product for defence expenditure, but we are aware that this percentage is unlikely to be achieved for several years. Therefore, despite the fact that the nominal amount of the budget has increased, on the other hand, we must also conclude that the share of defence expenditure in the gross domestic product has decreased to a problematic level of 0,97 %. At this point, I must make it clear that unmanned aircraft are not subject to procurement in 2015, nor are the funds provided for in the balance sheet you accept, nor are they an integral part of the real estate plan you will be considering tomorrow. Unmanned aircraft are defined in the Development Programme Plan adopted by the previous government in 2013. According to those plans, it is expected to be delivered in 2016-2017. What it's gonna be like to get these kind of planes, no one knows right now. Of course, they depend on the budgetary resources and also on the priorities we will set. No procedures have been carried out in connection with the purchase itself so far and no invitation to tender has yet been published. I'd like to say a few more words about the vehicles. True, at the Ministry of Defence we have a thousand 540 vehicles, the average age of these vehicles is 14 years and an average of 150,000 kilometres. With the vehicle replacement plan we planned a replacement of 272 vehicles, but this does not only mean replacing these vehicles, it also means disinvesting. With this, we will reduce the rolling stock by a full 20%, so that the cost of purchasing these vehicles will be borne by the means that would otherwise be used to service and repair old vehicles that would be used for fuel for old vehicles and also from resale itself. We have also informed the Committee of the National Defence Council, which has also agreed to such a programme. With our replacement of vehicles we will achieve rationalisation and also modernisation, we will reduce costs. It is worth noting that this move will reduce and not increase the number of vehicles in use, and the benefits will be greater. Thank you very much.

In addition, the state budget provides a transfer of billions and a half, which is 72 million less than last year; but still 52 million more than in the adopted budget. According to estimates prepared together with representatives of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office, we estimate that through this transfer we will be able to cover all liabilities to pensioners or to beneficiaries of other pension benefits. Let me point out once again that the reduction in resources to the budget user of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities for 130 million does not mean a reduction in the level of realisation in 2014, but rather a reduction in the estimates of the necessary expenditure, as we estimated in November 2013. It is therefore projects that have never had the European funds provided, but in some ways were waiting lines for the possible use of resources that would remain. Since spending rights have been allocated to draw back the old financial perspective more than European budget allocations, a scheme has been agreed with the municipalities involved in these 11 projects to prioritise these projects in drawing on European funds for a new cohesion or a new financial framework. However, in order to actually draw on the European funds of new financial cohesion, certain formal conditions must be met. The first condition is fulfilled, which means that the operational programme is signed. The second condition is to draw up implementing rules and these are in the final phase of inter-sectoral coordination. Furthermore, we need to be confirmed by the European Commission for the bodies that will take care of the implementation of the use of the funds of the new financial perspective. It is realistic to expect the first contracts to be signed somewhere in the middle of this year. Until then, projects cannot be named or listed in the budget rebalancing but can be defined as registered projects in the total amount. And, as I mentioned earlier, funds for this purpose are planned in two sub-programmes – water management and management or action in the field of climate change. With regard to the fear that we will not be able to draw on all the European funds of the old perspective, let me reiterate once again that the measure of the so-called surplus rights to use the allocated spending rights for the greater value of projects, such as the value of the whole envelope or the total amount of resources that can be expected from the European budget. And it's a normal way of planning that when you're done pumping, you can immediately replace a possible outage with new projects. All these projects are being developed, implemented and must be completed by the end of this year in order to be eligible for payment from European funds. Many of our efforts are also focused on this. Furthermore, the amount of funds allocated to the various agencies and the inter-ministerial working groups has been exposed. Transfers to budgets or transfers from the state budget to public agencies have decreased from EUR 137 million in 2007 to 29 million this year; in short, a drastic reduction in transfers to various agencies. Second. However, these inter-ministerial working groups do not have their own employees, but are involved in them by public servants. A whole set of objectives has been mentioned, a coalition treaty has been cited, but I believe that we all know that the coalition agreement has been concluded for 4 years; not for the whole budget document. With regard to the rise in taxes and the change in the income scale, these measures allow for the maintenance of tax revenues at the level that was already achieved in last year; and this is an extension of certain measures that have been in force so far. With regard to the allegations that we are reducing social transfers, let me stress once again that it is not a matter of any reduction of individual rights under this heading, but rather of the amount of resources we need to implement the legal obligations in this area. As a result of all the measures mentioned today, the estimated volume of this consumption is different in this year. Many negotiations or agreements have been mentioned. However, in SMC, we are convinced that the balance is a reflection of the hard work of the government towards a better tomorrow. The balance ahead of us, as the Prime Minister has already said, has pursued two objectives: promoting economic growth and increasing employment. Despite all the criticisms we are hearing, we insist on the position that the balance sheet is investment oriented and oriented. Let me re-emphasize what has already been heard today; the funds devoted to investment are increasing by almost €357 million, 30% more than in 2014. In 2015, the country will devote 5.5% of gross social product to public investment, which is stronger than the average in the euro area and the European Union. A detailed review of the budget shows that, compared to last year's expenditure in the balance sheet, significantly more resources are allocated to intervention programmes and commitments, these funds increase by 134 %. For environmental protection and environmental infrastructure, funds increase by 67%, local self-government increases by 60%, as well as health care, where funds increase by 23%. The 2015 budget balance provides for larger investment projects in the following areas: rail transport for a total of €471 million. In the area of roads and motorways total 29.5 million euros. In the area of water and wastewater treatment, a total of €152 million will be allocated to flood safety, €82.8 million will be spent in the field of health, all emergency centres will be completed in 2015, and investments will be made in the UKC Maribor, which is provided for in the 52.5 million euro balance sheet. In the area of information infrastructure, several major projects will be implemented totalling EUR 50.5 million. With this latter, the Mira Cerar government is following its commitments under the coalition treaty on the centralisation of the state information system, which will in the long run help to reduce the costs that the state is currently earmarking. As the government devotes 82.8 million resources to the necessary regulation of flood security, we also demonstrate that all amendments tabled at the time of the adoption of the 2014 balance were unnecessary and rejected on a reasonable basis and that the government has kept the word and provided the promised funds. In the discussion of the former Prime Minister, individual very low budget items were explicitly exposed, particularly those with only €48.98. Let me answer you, Mrs Bratušek, it is the payments made to the engineer and to the consulting services for the work carried out in 2014, with the recipient of the bulk of the assets being a state-owned enterprise, namely DRI investment management, d. o. o. In past discussions, it has also been clear to us that the budget balance is a major disappointment and that there is no new investment in it. True, it is mainly about continuing investments in progress or investments that are already known and for many of them the past governments have not found funds, as otherwise they would have already been completed. Of course, a new government cannot start, within six months of being in power, with large new projects that take time to implement. From design to the acquisition of all permits and the start of implementation, it cannot be only six months. We all know that. At this point, however, it should be pointed out that the Government of Mir Cerar sent an operational programme for the implementation of the European Cohesion Policy, which was also confirmed on 16 December, immediately after its appearance in Brussels. Why am I bringing this up? Since this document is the basis for all new projects which will undoubtedly be included in the 2016 budget, the preparations of which will start in the current year. These projects will in fact reflect the work of the Government of Mir Cerar. Thank you.
