POSITION OF THE DAY’S LINE, THIS IS A DEFINITIVE PROPOSAL OF THE AMENDMENT OF THE AMENDMENTS AND SUPPORTMENTS OF THE LAW ON THE IMPLEMENTATION OF THE BUDGETS OF THE REPUBLIC OF SLOVENIA FOR 2014 AND 2015. The draft law was tabled by the Government. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, Madam Secretary Vraničar.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u202	Madam Vice-President, ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Implementation of the Budgets of the Republic of Slovenia is usually accompanied by the State's budget rebalancing and sets out the solutions necessary for the smooth implementation of the budget. The current law on the implementation of the budget was adopted in 2013 together with the current budget for 2015. The law has been amended several times, and this time we propose the following amendments to the proposed balance. We propose an amendment to Article 12 of the current law, which allows the State to additionally be in charge of obligations in the pension protection policy and public debt service policy in the current year without providing an additional balance. We estimate that the estimates of spending on these two policies with the budgetary rebalance are fairly accurate and that we do not need such additional safeguards, and thus reduce the possibility of overcoming the deficit target by additional borrowing. We further propose the addition of Article 20, which is linked to the start of drawing on the European funds of the new financial perspective, by allowing the government's service for cohesion policy and development to self-distribute the funds for this pump. We are also limiting the possibility of concluding multi-annual contracts in a way that does not limit us to budgetary planning for the following years by taking on too much responsibility too quickly. We further propose an amendment to Article 34 of the Law in order to allow for the opening of integrated budget lines during the year, where this is necessary for the recovery of unused or unduly used funds from the European budget or other international financial mechanisms. Further, we are extending the possibility of the administrator of the country's Single Treasury Account, with Article 6, to pursue an appropriate investment policy with regard to the charging of free funds. With the proposed amendment, we will allow the operator to invest free money also in the European monetary union savings and state treasures and in the purchase of the treasury of these countries under certain conditions. In accordance with the balance sheet, the extent of the maximum possible indebtedness of the country in this year should be adjusted in Article 7. However, Articles 8, 9 and 10 lay down rules for transfers from the State budget to municipal budgets. Article 8 thus specifies the average for this year in accordance with the agreement with the representative municipal associations. It is further laid down that, from 1 January 2015 onwards, municipalities shall be provided with income and financial compensation up to the amount of eligible expenditure. Article 10 provides for the amount of the investment transfer referred to in Article 21 of the Law on the Financing of Municipalities. The amended law further proposes that municipalities should be allowed additional responsibility for co-financing European cohesion projects, which was also one of the requirements set by the municipalities during the talks on the average of this year. Taking into account this content, we propose that the National Assembly should support the proposal for a law in both the second and third readings. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u203	Thank you. The draft law was treated by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy as a parent labour force. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to Mr Tiln Bozic, Vice-President.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u204	Your Honor, good evening! Report of the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy as a parent working body in the case of the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Implementation of the Budgets of the Republic of Slovenia for 2014 and 2015 by the meeting of 17. 2. 2015 is relatively extensive, so I will only take it meaningfully below. In the context of the material for the meeting, the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, the opinion of the Commission of the State Council on Economic Affairs, Crafts, Tourism and Finance, supported by the proposal for a law, the opinion of the Commission of the National Assembly on the supervision of public finances as an interested working body, was forwarded to the Committee. Several amendments have been tabled to the amendment, namely: NSIs to Articles 8 and 10, United Left to Article 1, SDS to Articles 6 and 7, SMC, Desus and SD coalition groups to Articles 1, 3, 6 and 9 and First-signed Mr Jurša and the other Members Desus and SD to Article 1 and to Article 10a. At the meeting, a proposal for amendments to Article 9 was also tabled at the initiative of representatives of the ZPS and the Government. The meeting was attended by a representative of the Legal and Legislative Service, representatives of the Ministry of Finance and in conclusion of the hearing also by the Minister of Finance, Dr Dušan Maramor. Following an introductory explanation of the new solutions proposed by the Secretary of the Ministry of Finance, the representative of the Legal and Legislative Service explained that the comments made in the written opinion were largely taken into account by the amendments of the coalition groups. In the course of the debate, in the context of the decision on the articles and the amendments tabled, representatives of the parliamentary groups that tabled the amendments to each article presented, on behalf of the proposers, a substantive presentation of the amendments. The discussion focused on paragraphs 1, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10 and the newly proposed 10a. If I briefly sum up the debates. With regard to Article 1 and the possibility of indemnity for payment of debts and pension payments, the United Nations Group of the Left has proposed the deletion, as they believe that the payment of pensions is directly at risk. The Desus Group and the Social Democrats have proposed a less restrictive adaptation of the provision. The representative of the Government explained that the Government's proposal does not reduce the possibility of payment of pensions provided by the income of the pension fund, from contributions, other income from the pension fund or from the transfer from the state budget to the pension fund. With regard to Article 6 on the deposit of free funds in the store, the SDS Group expressed its opposition to the imposition of free funds into the store and proposed the deletion. In their view, investments in savings banks are more risky than bank investments. Representatives of coalition parties stressed the optimisation of the availability of funds, costs and perhaps slightly higher returns. The representative of the Government explained that in the case of savings facilities, according to the capital adequacy and investment policy, it is a comparable risk and would be inappropriate to exclude savings. With regard to Article 7, as regards the possibility of additional State indebtedness, the SDS Group stressed that the provision increases the possibility of debt representing a common debt. The representative of the Government rejected the statement by explaining that it was a possible and not an actual level of indebtedness and provided data on the extent of pre-financing and current indebtedness. With regard to Article 8 which sets the average for municipalities, the NSI Group pointed out that this was unacceptable in their view, since interference at the level of averages downwards could jeopardise the performance of the duties of municipalities and what has already been prescribed by the Law on the Financing of Communities. With regard to the possibility of additional indebtedness of municipalities, the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service expressed concerns about the unclear manner and procedure for granting the consent of the competent ministry to the additional indebtedness of the municipalities, thus triggering a debate on the possibility of submitting an amendment to the amendment that would remedy this deficiency. With regard to Article 10 and the reduction of municipal investment funds, the NSI Group expressed its view that the reduction of investment resources is not acceptable to municipalities. The government rejected this by explaining that this was in line with the representatives of the municipalities. The proposed Article 10a proposing the coordination of pensions was also of interest. The Minister of Finance strongly opposed such an amendment. Let me finish with the bottom line. The Committee has considered the amendments of the coalition parliamentary groups. The amendments to Articles 1, 3, 6 and 9 were adopted and the other amendments were rejected. The Committee also voted on all the articles of the bill together at the end and adopted them. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u205	Thank you. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. The word is given by the Group of Social Democrats, Dr. Franz the Crusader.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u206	Dear President, representatives of the government, distinguished High Council! The proposal for a novel Law on the implementation of the budgets of the Republic of Slovenia for 2014 and 2015 contains some urgent solutions that will enable the current and smooth implementation of the budget balance of the Republic of Slovenia in 2015. Let us recall that, among other things, the aim is to reduce the budget deficit below the Maastricht limit of 3%. In addition to the revenue side measures that were adopted by the majority at the end of last year, the government has reached some important agreements in the negotiations that will shorten the expenditure side of the budget. Thus, at the end of last December, the government concluded an agreement with the trade unions on measures, weighing more than 300 million euros. For its immediate public financial impact, it has agreed to release progress and to start negotiations on the elimination of salary anomalies, changes in the pay system and wage policy in the public sector. The proposed amendment to the law on the implementation of the budget shall, inter alia, legislate the content of one of the tough nuts which the Government has resolved or broken down in the negotiations, this time with representatives of local communities. In this way, tomorrow in the vote, we will legislate solutions from the content of the agreement between the Government and the associations of municipalities on the average and sequestering of investment transfers. The average will thus be €525 by 30 June and €500.83 by the end of the year from 1 July to the end of the year. The financial impact of the measure is around €40 million and, together with a percentage reduction in investment and the elimination of the solidarity balance, the expenditure side of the budget is easier for around €60 million. That's the load. We point out, however, that the Government must, in accordance with the agreement adopted by the end of June, prepare and ensure the adoption of systemic measures to reduce the costs of municipalities at an annual level of almost €23 million. This is now a new hard nut for the government, which will have to be crushed again. An important part of the novel proposal is also an increase in the country's debt threshold by about €1.3 billion. In this regard, we note that there is a double effect. Slovenia is extremely liquid. Representatives of the Government have informed us that we have 4 billion and a half liquid assets that the state can use at any time or in due course if any measure is to be financed. This, of course, is due to the great financial crisis and then to the speculative attack in 2012 and 2013. This security is even slightly increased by this additional debt, and representatives of the Government have assured us that these funds operate in a way that has further contributed to a reduction in the interest rate or a reduction in the return on Slovenian bonds. This shows us in the light of what we should have been shown before, it is said to be an export-oriented, successful, industrialised economy. Let's get to the next topic. A commitment must be opened, not only to the financial markets, but also to our people. These include the release of certain rights when economic growth exceeds a certain threshold, which is usually 2.5% of gross domestic product growth. If it turns out that this was indeed above mentioned last year, pensions will gradually be brought into line, as provided for by the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, followed by an increase in State grants and child allowances, parental compensation, a large family allowance and other social transfers. In the Group of Social Democrats, we expect the Government to provide funds through internal transfers in cases that show that the planned funds will not be able to meet the legal obligations. In the Group of Social Democrats on the Law on the Enforcement of Budgets, we draw attention to the consequences of the required reduction in the number of employees in the public sector by 1%. The flat-rate non-selective policy of public sector decommissioning is reflected in the sub-nutrition of certain parts of the system and is harmful to development and closes the employment space for young people. The proposal for a new law on the implementation of budgets for 2014 and 2015 will be supported by the Group of Social Democrats. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u207	Thank you. The position of the United Left Group will be presented by Luka Mesec.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u208	Thank you for your word. Nice hello to you all! Three things are laid down in this law. First, an increase in the maximum permitted level of indebtedness. Secondly, restrictions on investments and budgets of municipalities. And thirdly, the priorities of fiscal policy. Point one. The increase in the debt is reasonable and justified because in 2013 it was not possible to foresee additional expenditure on interest and similar expenditure for 2015. Secondly, we oppose restrictions on the investments and budgets of the municipalities. We are opposed to a linear reduction in the investment of municipalities by 1%, for two reasons. Firstly, this decrease is detrimental to economic growth and increases unemployment. Secondly, we do not agree to saving on investments and jobs in Slovenia, but at the same time the country finances, say, the construction of a new NATO headquarters. Thirdly, this brings us to the third question, the issue of priority. Article 12 of the Law on the implementation of the budgets of the Republic of Slovenia for 2014 and 2015. This Article provides, inter alia, that pension security obligations shall also be settled if there is no provision for them on the relevant items. The second paragraph of Article 12 allows pension protection obligations to be dealt with in the extreme case with by additional State indebtedness. However, the amendment explicitly excludes this option. This potentially jeopardises the payment of pensions. This would result in a reduction of social rights and an increase in the risk of poverty in pensioners. And that risk is real. Both the 2014 budget rebalancing and the 2015 budget rebalancing required an increase of more than EUR 100 million. What if something unpredictable happens in 2015 as well and there will be an additional responsibility for the payment of pensions? They're going to retire this year? ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u217	Thank you. Mr Tillen Bozič will present the position of the Parliamentary Group of the Mir Cerar Party.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u218	Hello again! In line with current practice, today, in addition to the 2015 budget rebalancing, we also have before us the amendment of the Law on the Implementation of the Budgets of the Republic of Slovenia for 2014 and 2015. This is a technical law that operationalises some of the measures set out in the 2015 budget balance. Let me give some key points of the novel. It is envisaged that the provision allowing additional debt for the purposes of covering pension protection obligations will cease to apply, as new projections have established and then, through the means of assessment, ensured that the measure in its existing form is no longer necessary. If, despite careful planning, there are unforeseen differences over the course of the year, however, there is still an open path to rescue through reallocation or, only in the extreme case, a rebalancing. The amendment to the law also reflects the updated way in which cohesion funds are allocated over the period 2014-2020. As of 2015, resources are no longer allocated on the basis of a rough conceptual cut, but they must be justified by concrete projects prior to deployment. For that reason, powers shall be extended to the Administrative Authority for Cohesion Policy in a manner allowing for later reallocation, between the Slovenian participation items and between the European Union earmarked items. The novel also includes solutions agreed in close cooperation by the Government with representative associations of municipalities with the Community of Slovene municipalities and the Association of Slovene municipalities. We have further or better settled some of the solutions with amendments already supported by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy. The key changes are: the adoption of the average for 2015, which is set at EUR 525 or just over EUR 500 for the second half of 2015; the reduction in the volume of funds for investments by municipalities from 2 % to 1%; and the measure last mentioned is the provision of money for investment projects by municipalities with an additional debt of EUR 750 000 per municipality, but not more than EUR 10 million in total for all municipalities. The tact in determining these amounts was necessary, as the indebtedness of the congregations also contributes to the identification of the common debt and the indirect deficit that must be kept under constant review in these times. The news is also an expansion of the range of possibilities for moving surplus funds to municipalities, as surpluses could now also be deposited in storage facilities. These are financial companies participating in Eurosystem open market operations that are specifically regulated and licensed for business and under the supervision of the central bank. For these reasons, we see no reason in the SMC to treat them differently in a particular case than business banks. The current discussions under the budget have already mentioned the regulation of the possibility of borrowing a country in an allegedly excessive amount. The determination of the extent of the country's permissible indebtedness under this Act follows consistently the deficits of the expenditure balance, A, the balance sheet of financial claims and investments, B, and the financing balance, C. It also takes into account the extent of pre-financing and current indebtedness. The amount, therefore, represents a mere alignment with the 2015 budget rebalancing that we are adopting. In the SMC Group, we believe that the amendment, taking into account the coalition-coordinated amendments already supported by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy, is prepared in accordance with our expectations. As a result, the members of the SMC Group support the amendment of the Law on the Implementation of the Budgets of the Republic of Slovenia for 2014 and 2015. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-02-19-SDZ7-Izredna-09.ana.u236	Thank you very much. This explanation of the amendment is somewhat unusual. And why this uncertainty? In addition to 50 million regular transfers for the coordination of pensions intended primarily for the coordination of pensions, Kad will transfer 140 million additional funds for the privatisation of state assets to the pension fund in addition to the 50 million regular transfers for the coordination of pensions. This privatisation did not take place in 2013. Therefore, this transfer from Kad to the pension fund was not available. Therefore, it is not a reason to change or otherwise implement the transfer from the state budget to the pension fund in the absence of accurate planning, but rather that what was envisaged for 2013 did not happen. Similarly, in 2014 it did not happen that Kad would have any revenue from the sale of state assets that he would have transferred to the pension register. In early 2014, there was a further amendment to the SDH Act, which abolished this provision and thereby reduced the pension allowance for transfers from Kad by 190 million. This missing transfer also had to be aligned with the needs of the pensioner’s cash register in 2014 through a rebalancing. However, the state budget must cover an additional 52 million this year. I think that's a fact, but on average, let's say those who have been retired have been working for 37 years, so they're not all at 40. We have many cases, even in the past, when some retired prematurely, even before 50 years ago. I don't even dream of it. I believe that in order to repay these debts and work on it, I will probably retire at the age of 70, if at all. It is true that the first time this pay as you go system was introduced, it is called the opposite coverage of pensions, the average age that someone could have experienced, about three years under the age that was envisaged for obtaining a pension. This system has changed so much with the upgrade of the social system and has improved so much that today we can actually afford someone who retires to survive then, let's say, relatively high-quality, at least as we would like, 20 years of life. In addition to the pensions we have, it is still necessary to say that not all pensions are so low, which in some ways gives me grief, because this means that there are very many under that average who receive extremely low pensions, but there are also many who receive pensions over a thousand euros, in some cases I have seen that over a thousand 700 euros. There is also little to think about in terms of solidarity, it is also said that solidarity is within a particular social structure, or a particular group of people, or maybe there is room for solidarity, I don't know. Furthermore, as regards the solidarity of young people or younger groups of people we talk about so many times. How much money does it give to young people? It's amazing. On the other hand, young people are also paying, including into the health system, and this is also about solidarity, redistribution of resources to those who need it, from those who do not currently need it. And then, if we're going to maybe some awkward things that I've had the chance to experience, what it's like to rent an apartment from a pensioner in these market conditions. Older people have much more wealth than the young population. There's solidarity here, too. I don't want to object or anything like that here, but when you look at it, you have to look at the whole picture, and when you get rid of those who really don't, you really focus on those who do, and you have to help them in every way. But I would like to point out that this does not apply to everything. So we're talking about coordination, a small one that weighs 5 billion euros. If I go to the other side, about this debt and about releasing the investments of the municipalities and so on.
